\begin{remarks}[19.5.6]
\label{IV.19.5.6}
\medskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item[\rm(i)] The results of this number remain valid when, instead of an $\mathrm{A}$-module $\mathrm{M}$ and elements $f_i\in\mathrm{A}$, one takes an object $\mathrm{M}$ of any abelian category, and $n$ endomorphisms $f_i$ of $\mathrm{M}$ that mutually commute; the demonstrations adapt without difficulty to this more general case. The hypothesis of separation for an $\mathrm{A}$-module $\mathrm{N}$ relative to the $\mathfrak{J}$-preadic topology must here be replaced by the hypothesis that every sub-object of $\mathrm{N}$ contained in all the $\sum f_i^r(\mathrm{N})$ ($r$ an arbitrary integer) is necessarily zero. The same remark applies to the results of \sref{IV.19.7}.
\item[\rm(ii)] Suppose that $\mathrm{A}$ is a \emph{graded} ring in degrees $\geq0$. If $\mathrm{M}$ (resp. each $\operatorname{gr}_p(\mathrm{M})$) is a graded $\mathrm{A}$-module with degrees bounded below, and if the $f_i$ contain no homogeneous component of degree $0$, the separation hypothesis of $(\mathbf{0},15.1.9)$ is \emph{ipso facto} satisfied for $\mathrm{M}$ (resp. each $\operatorname{gr}_p(\mathrm{M})$), and one sees that in this case we have the implication \emph{c)}$\Rightarrow$\emph{a)} in \sref{IV.19.5.1} (resp. \sref{IV.19.5.5}).
\item[\rm(iii)] Recall that, without the separation hypothesis, the implication \emph{c)}$\Rightarrow$\emph{b)} is no longer valid even for $n=1$ $(\mathbf{0},15.1.12,\text{ (iii)})$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{remarks}

\subsection{Regular sequences relative to a filtered quotient module}
\label{subsection:IV.19.6}

\begin{env}[19.6.1]
\label{IV.19.6.1}
Let $\mathrm{A}$ be a ring, $\mathbf{f}=(f_1,\ldots,f_n)$ a finite sequence of elements of $\mathrm{A}$, $\mathfrak{J}=f_1\mathrm{A}+\ldots+f_n\mathrm{A}$ the ideal it generates, and consider an exact sequence of $\mathrm{A}$-modules
\[
  0\to\mathrm{R}\xrightarrow{j}\mathrm{N}\xrightarrow{p}\mathrm{M}\to 0
\]
where one supposes that $\mathrm{N}$ is endowed with a decreasing filtration $(\mathrm{N}_k)$ of sub-$\mathrm{A}$-modules, with $\mathrm{N}_0=\mathrm{N}$. We set $\mathrm{R}_k=\mathrm{R}\cap\mathrm{N}_k$ (filtration induced by $(\mathrm{N}_k)$), $\mathrm{M}_k=p(\mathrm{N}_k)$ (quotient filtration of $(\mathrm{N}_k)$) and we denote by $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{N})$, $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{R})$ and $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{M})$ the graded $\mathrm{A}$-modules associated with these three filtrations. We set on the other hand for all $k$,
\[
  \mathrm{N}_k'=\mathrm{N}_k+\mathfrak{J}\mathrm{N}_{k-1}+\ldots+\mathfrak{J}^{k-1}\mathrm{N}_1+\mathfrak{J}^k\mathrm{N}_0
\]
so that $\mathrm{M}_k'=p(\mathrm{N}_k')=\mathrm{M}_k+\mathfrak{J}\mathrm{M}_{k-1}+\ldots+\mathfrak{J}^{k-1}\mathrm{M}_1+\mathfrak{J}^k\mathrm{M}_0$; we set on the other hand $\mathrm{R}_k'=\mathrm{R}\cap\mathrm{N}_k'$ (filtration \emph{induced} by $(\mathrm{N}_k')$), and we denote by $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(\mathrm{N})$, $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(\mathrm{M})$ and $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(\mathrm{R})$ the graded $\mathrm{A}$-modules associated with these three filtrations; we thus have a commutative diagram of exact sequences
\[
\xymatrix{
  0 \ar[r] & \operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{R}) \ar[r]^-{\operatorname{gr}(j)} \ar[d] & \operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{N}) \ar[r]^-{\operatorname{gr}(p)} \ar[d] & \operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{M}) \ar[r] \ar[d] & 0 \\
  0 \ar[r] & \operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(\mathrm{R}) \ar[r]^-{\operatorname{gr}'(j)} & \operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(\mathrm{N}) \ar[r]^-{\operatorname{gr}'(p)} & \operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(\mathrm{M}) \ar[r] & 0
}
\tag{19.6.1.1}
\label{IV.19.6.1.1}
\]

\begin{env}[19.5.5.2]
\label{IV.19.5.5.2}
\emph{Pour que la suite $\mathbf f$ soit $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(M)$-quasi-régulière, il suffit que, pour $p\geq0$ et $q\geq0$, la condition suivante soit vérifiée:}

$(\mathrm S_{q,p})$ \emph{Pour toute famille $(x_{\mathbf q}^p)_{|\mathbf q|=q}$ d'éléments de $M_p$, la relation $\sum_{|\mathbf q|=q}\mathbf f^{\mathbf q}x_{\mathbf q}^p\in M_{p+1}$ implique $x_{\mathbf q}^p\in M_{p+1}+\mathfrak JM_p$ pour tout $\mathbf q$ tel que $|\mathbf q|=q$.}

Par définition, dire que $\mathbf f$ est $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(M)$-quasi-régulière signifie que, pour tout $p\geq0$ et tout $q\geq0$, une relation
\[
  \sum_{|\mathbf q|=q}\mathbf f^{\mathbf q}x_{\mathbf q}^p\in M_{p+1}+\mathfrak J^{q+1}M_p
\]

\begin{env}[19.6.2]
\label{IV.19.6.2}
We defined in $(\mathbf{0},15.1.5.2)$ the surjective graded homomorphisms of degree $0$
\begin{align*}
  \psi_\mathrm{N} &: (\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{N})\otimes_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J}))[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_n] \to \operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(\mathrm{N}) \\
  \psi_\mathrm{M} &: (\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{M})\otimes_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J}))[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_n] \to \operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(\mathrm{M}) \\
  \psi_\mathrm{R} &: (\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{R})\otimes_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J}))[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_n] \to \operatorname{gr}_\bullet''(\mathrm{R})
\end{align*}
the first two of which are deduced from the last two vertical arrows of \sref{IV.19.6.1.1}.

As the filtration $(\mathrm{R}_k'')$ is finer than $(\mathrm{R}_k')$, on $\mathrm{R}$ we have a canonical homomorphism $\gamma:\operatorname{gr}_\bullet''(\mathrm{R})\to\operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(\mathrm{R})$; we will denote by $\psi_\mathrm{R}'$ the composite homomorphism $\gamma\circ\psi_\mathrm{R}$. It then follows immediately from the definitions that we have a commutative diagram
\[
\xymatrix{
  & 0 \ar[d] \\
  (\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{R})\otimes_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J}))[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_n] \ar[r]^-{\psi_\mathrm{R}'} \ar[d]_{j'} & \operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(\mathrm{R}) \ar[d]^{\operatorname{gr}'(j)} \\
  (\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{N})\otimes_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J}))[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_n] \ar[r]^-{\psi_\mathrm{N}} \ar[d]_{p'} & \operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(\mathrm{N}) \ar[d]^{\operatorname{gr}'(p)} \\
  (\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{M})\otimes_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J}))[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_n] \ar[r]^-{\psi_\mathrm{M}} \ar[d] & \operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(\mathrm{M}) \ar[d] \\
  0 & 0
}
\tag{19.6.2.1}
\label{IV.19.6.2.1}
\]
where the columns are exact.
\end{env}

\item[\rm e)] $\psi_\mathrm{R}'$ is bijective.
\item[\rm f)] $j'$ is injective.

\subsection{Suites régulières relativement à un module filtré quotient.}
\EGAReaderTarget{ega4.c04.subsec.19-6}{subsection:IV.19.6}

\begin{env}[19.6.1]
\label{IV.19.6.1}
Soient $A$ un anneau, $\mathbf f=(f_1,\ldots,f_n)$ une s

[id 22371]

NEW: \ar[r]_-{\operatorname{gr}'(j)} & \operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(N) \ar[r]_-{\operatorname{gr}'(p)}
FRENCH CONTEXT (corrected): ion}
\xymatrix{
  0 \ar[r] & \operatorname{gr}_\bullet(R) \ar[r]^-{\operatorname{gr}(j)} \ar[d] & \operatorname{gr}_\bullet(N) \ar[r]^-{\operatorname{gr}(p)} \ar[d] & \operatorname{gr}_\bullet(M) \ar[r] \ar[d] & 0 \\
  0 \ar[r] & \operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(R) \ar[r]_-{\operatorname{gr}'(j)} & \operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(N) \ar[r]_-{\operatorname{gr}'(p)} & \operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(M) \ar[r] & 0
}
\tag{19.6.1.1}\label{IV.19.6.1.1}
\end{equation}
\oldpage[IV-4]{210}
où les flèches verticales sont les homomorphismes canoniques d'un module gradué associé à une filtration dans le module gradué associé à une filt

=================== PAGE IV-4 210 ===================
----- (English page IV-4 209: shown above in this file) -----
----- (English page IV-4 210: shown above in this file) -----
----- English, source_aligned/ega4-19.tex, page IV-4 211 -----
\oldpage[IV-4]{211}
\item[\rm \textup{f$'$)}] The homomorphism $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{R})\otimes_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J})\to\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{N})\otimes_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J})$ obtained by restricting $j'$ to polynomials of degree $0$, is injective.
\item[\rm g)] The $\mathfrak{J}$-preadic filtration of $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{R})$ is induced by that of $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{N})$.
\item[\rm h)] The filtrations $(\mathrm{R}_k')$ and $(\mathrm{R}_k'')$ on $\mathrm{R}$ are identical.
\end{enumerate}
Then we have the following implications:
\[
\xymatrix@C=2.4em@R=1.8em{
  \text{a)} \ar@{=>}[r] \ar@{=>}[dr] &
  \text{e)} \ar@{=>}[r] \ar@{=>}[dr] &
  \text{b)} \ar@{<=>}[r] &
  \text{c)} \ar@{<=>}[r] &
  \text{h)} \\
  & \text{g)} \ar@{=>}[r] &
  \text{d)} \ar@{<=>}[r] &
  \text{d$'$)} \ar@{<=>}[r] &
  \text{f)} \ar@{<=>}[r] &
  \text{f$'$)}
}
\]

Moreover, when every quotient module of a submodule of $\operatorname{gr}_k(\mathrm{M})$ is separated for the $\mathfrak{J}$-preadic topology (which is the case when $\mathrm{A}$ is Noetherian, the $f_i$ belong to the radical of $\mathrm{A}$, and the $\operatorname{gr}_k(\mathrm{M})$ are finitely generated $\mathrm{A}$-modules), \emph{then all conditions} \emph{a)} to \emph{h)} \emph{are equivalent}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Let us note that, from \sref{IV.19.5.5}, the hypothesis that $\mathbf{f}$ is $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{N})$-regular implies that $\psi_\mathrm{N}$ is \emph{bijective}; as $\operatorname{gr}'(j)$ is injective, the equivalence of $d)$ and $f)$ results from the diagram \sref{IV.19.6.2.1}. Likewise $d')$ and $f')$ are equivalent, while $f)$ and $f')$ are trivially equivalent; this proves the equivalence of $d)$, $d')$, $f)$ and $f')$.

As one already knows that $\psi_\mathrm{M}$ is surjective and $\psi_\mathrm{N}$ bijective, the equivalence of $b)$ and $c)$ also follows from the diagram \sref{IV.19.6.2.1} by a particular case of the five lemma.

The relation $\psi_\mathrm{R}'=\gamma\circ\psi_\mathrm{R}$ \sref{IV.19.6.2} and the fact that $\psi_\mathrm{R}$ is surjective show that condition $c)$ is equivalent to saying that $\gamma$ is surjective, which is also equivalent to condition $h)$; hence we have proved the equivalence of $b)$, $c)$ and $h)$.

It is trivial that $e)$ is equivalent to the conjunction of $c)$ and $d)$, and hence also of $b)$ and $d)$. Let us note now that $a)$ implies $b)$ and that $b)$ implies $a)$ under the separation hypothesis \sref{IV.19.5.5}. On the other hand, the implications $a)\Rightarrow g)\Rightarrow f')$, and the implication $f')\Rightarrow a)$ under the separation hypothesis, result from \sref{IV.19.5.3}. Hence we have proved $a)$ implies $e)$ (equivalent to the conjunction of $b)$ and $f')$), and also that all conditions are equivalent under the separation hypothesis.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[19.6.4]
\label{IV.19.6.4}
Let $\mathfrak K$ be an ideal of $\mathrm{A}$, $\mathfrak{L}=\mathfrak{J}+\mathfrak K$. Suppose that the filtration $(\mathrm{N}_k)$ (hence also $(\mathrm{M}_k)$) is the $\mathfrak K$-preadic filtration, so that the filtrations $(\mathrm{M}_k')$ and $(\mathrm{N}_k')$ are $\mathfrak{L}$-preadic filtrations. Consider $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{R})$ (resp. $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(\mathrm{R})$) as a graded module over $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^*(\mathrm{A})$ (resp. $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{L}^*(\mathrm{A})$).
\begin{enumerate}
\item[\rm(i)] Let $\mathrm{S}$ be a part of $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{R})$ that generates $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{R})$ as a $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^*(\mathrm{A})$-module. Then condition \emph{c)} of \emph{(\sref{IV.19.6.3})} is equivalent to the following condition:
\item[\rm \textup{c$'$)}] The image of $\mathrm{S}$ in $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(\mathrm{R})$ by $\psi_\mathrm{R}'$ generates $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(\mathrm{R})$ as a $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{L}^*(\mathrm{A})$-module.
\item[\rm(ii)] Suppose condition \emph{e)} of \emph{(\sref{IV.19.6.3})} is satisfied, and also that the quotients of $\operatorname{gr}_k(\mathrm{R})$ are separated for the $\mathfrak{J}$-preadic topology (which is the case when $\mathrm{A}$ is Noetherian, the $f_i$ belong to the radical of $\mathrm{A}$, and the $\operatorname{gr}_k(\mathrm{N})$ are finitely generated $\mathrm{A}$-modules). Then, \emph{for a part $\mathrm{S}$ of $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{R})$ formed of homogeneous elements to generate $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{R})$ as a $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^*(\mathrm{A})$-module, it is necessary and sufficient that its image by $\psi_\mathrm{R}'$ generates $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(\mathrm{R})$ as a $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{L}^*(\mathrm{A})$-module.}
\end{enumerate}
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}

\end{env}

\begin{env}[19.6.2]

  \item[\textup{c)}] $\psi_R'$ est surjectif \textup{(autrement dit $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(R)$ est engendré comme $\operatorname{gr}_{\mathfrak J}^\bullet(A)$-module par $\operatorname{Im}(\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(R)\to\operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(R))$)}.
  \item[\textup{d)}] $\psi_R'$ est injectif.
  \item[\textup{d$'$)}] L'homomorphisme $(\psi_R')_0:\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(R)\otimes_A(A/\mathfrak J)\to\operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(R)$ obtenu en restreignant $\psi_R'$ aux polynômes de degré $0$, est injectif.

----- (English page IV-4 211: shown above in this file) -----

\oldpage[IV-4]{212}
\begin{enumerate}
\item[\rm(i)] Consider the homomorphism of degree $0$
\[
  \psi_\mathrm{A}:(\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^*(\mathrm{A})\otimes_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J}))[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_n]\to\operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(\mathrm{A})=\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{L}^*(\mathrm{A})
\]
$(\mathbf{0},15.1.5.2)$. The fact that this homomorphism is \emph{surjective} implies that the sub-$\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{L}^*(\mathrm{A})$-module of $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(\mathrm{R})$ generated by $\mathrm{S}'=\psi_\mathrm{R}'(\mathrm{S})$ is none other than $\operatorname{Im}(\psi_\mathrm{R}')$, whence the equivalence of $c)$ and $c')$.

\item[\rm(ii)] Since condition $e)$ is satisfied, it follows by (i) that it remains to prove the sufficiency of the condition in the statement. Now, as $\psi_\mathrm{R}'$ is bijective, to say that $\mathrm{S}'$ generates $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(\mathrm{R})$ considered as a $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{L}^*(\mathrm{A})$-module amounts to saying that $\mathrm{S}$ generates $(\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{R})\otimes_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J}))[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_n]$ as a $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{L}^*(\mathrm{A})$-module, and by the surjectivity of $\psi_\mathrm{A}$, we can in this statement replace the ring $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{L}^*(\mathrm{A})$ by $(\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^*(\mathrm{A})\otimes_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J}))[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_n]$. It amounts to saying that $\mathrm{S}$ generates $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{R})\otimes_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J})$ as a module over the ring $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^*(\mathrm{A})\otimes_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J})$, or also as a $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^*(\mathrm{A})$-module. Now, if $\mathrm{T}$ is the graded sub-$\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^*(\mathrm{A})$-module of $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{R})$ generated by $\mathrm{S}$, and if we set $\mathrm{T}'=\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{R})/\mathrm{T}$, then by hypothesis $\mathrm{T}'/\mathfrak{J}\mathrm{T}'=0$, or $\mathfrak{J}\mathrm{T}'=\mathrm{T}'$; but $\mathrm{T}'$ is a graded module, and each $\mathrm{T}_p'$ is a quotient of a submodule of $\operatorname{gr}_p(\mathrm{R})$, hence separated for the $\mathfrak{J}$-preadic topology. The hypothesis therefore implies $\mathrm{T}'=0$, which completes the proof of the corollary.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\subsection{Hironaka's normal flatness criterion}
\label{subsection:IV.19.7}
\label{IV.19.7}

\begin{theorem}[19.7.1]
\label{IV.19.7.1}
\textup{(Hironaka).}
Let $\mathrm{A}$ be a ring, $\mathfrak K$ an ideal of $\mathrm{A}$, $\mathbf{f}=(f_1,\ldots,f_n)$ a sequence of elements of $\mathrm{A}$ that is $(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak K)$-regular, $\mathfrak{J}=f_1\mathrm{A}+\ldots+f_n\mathrm{A}$ the ideal generated by $\mathbf{f}$, $\mathfrak{L}=\mathfrak{J}+\mathfrak K$, $\mathrm{M}$ an $\mathrm{A}$-module.
In this case $(\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^*(\mathrm{M}))\otimes_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J})=(\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^*(\mathrm{M}))\otimes_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{L})=(\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^*(\mathrm{M}))\otimes_{\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak K}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{L})$, since $(\mathfrak K^n\mathrm{M}/\mathfrak K^{n+1}\mathrm{M})\otimes_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J})=\mathfrak K^n\mathrm{M}/(\mathfrak K^{n+1}+\mathfrak{J}\mathfrak K^n)\mathrm{M}=\mathfrak K^n\mathrm{M}/(\mathfrak K^{n+1}+\mathfrak{L}\mathfrak K^n)\mathrm{M}$, and the last equality is a result of Bourbaki, \emph{Alg.}, chap.~II, $3^e$~ed., \textsection~3, n\textsuperscript{o}~7, cor.~2 of prop.~6.

Consider the following conditions:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[\rm a)] $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^*(\mathrm{M})$ is a flat $(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak K)$-module.
\item[\rm b)] $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{L}^*(\mathrm{M})$ is a flat $(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{L})$-module and the canonical homomorphism $(\mathbf{0},15.1.5.2)$
\[
  \psi_\mathrm{M}:((\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^*(\mathrm{M}))\otimes_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{L}))[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_n]\to\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{L}^*(\mathrm{M})
\]
is bijective.
\item[\rm c)] $(\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^*(\mathrm{M}))\otimes_{\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak K}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{L})$ is a flat $(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{L})$-module and the sequence $\mathbf{f}$ is $(\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^*(\mathrm{M}))$-regular.
\item[\rm d)] $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{L}^*(\mathrm{M})$ is a flat $(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{L})$-module, and for every $\mathfrak{p}\in\operatorname{Ass}_{\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak K}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{L})$, $(\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^*(\mathrm{M}))_\mathfrak{p}$ is a flat $(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak K)_\mathfrak{p}$-module.
\end{enumerate}
Then we have the implications
\[
\xymatrix@C=3em@R=1.6em{
  \text{a)} \ar@{=>}[r] \ar@{=>}[dr] & \text{c)} \ar@{=>}[r] & \text{b)} \\
  & \text{d)}
}
\]

When every quotient of a submodule of $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^k(\mathrm{M})$ $(k\geqslant 0)$ is ideally separated for $\mathfrak{J}$ (Bourbaki, \emph{Alg.~comm.}, chap.~III, \textsection~5, n\textsuperscript{o}~1), the conditions \emph{a)}, \emph{b)}, \emph{c)} are equivalent.

\oldpage[IV-4]{212}

\begin{enumerate}
  \item[(i)] Considérons l'homomorphisme de degré $0$
  \[
    \psi_A:(\operatorname{gr}_{\mathfrak K}^\bullet(A)\otimes_A(A/\mathfrak J))[T_1,\ldots,T_n]\longrightarrow\operatorname{gr}_

=================== PAGE IV-4 212 ===================
----- (English page IV-4 211: shown above in this file) -----
----- (English page IV-4 212: shown above in this file) -----
----- English, source_aligned/ega4-19.tex, page IV-4 213 -----
\oldpage[IV-4]{213}
Finally, when $\mathrm{A}$ is Noetherian, the $f_i$ belong to the radical of $\mathrm{A}$, the sequence $\mathbf{f}$ is $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^*(\mathrm{A})$-regular, and $\mathrm{M}$ is a finitely generated $\mathrm{A}$-module, the conditions \emph{a)}, \emph{b)}, \emph{c)}, \emph{d)} are equivalent.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The implication \emph{a)} $\Rightarrow$ \emph{c)} is immediate $(\mathbf{0},15.1.13)$. If $c)$ is satisfied, it results from \sref{IV.19.5.5} that $\psi_\mathrm{M}$ is bijective; moreover, $(\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^*(\mathrm{M})\otimes_{\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak K}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{L}))[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_n]$ is a flat $(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{L})$-module, and hence so is $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{L}^*(\mathrm{M})$, since $\psi_\mathrm{M}$ is an $(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{L})$-isomorphism; thus $c)$ implies $b)$. The fact that $a)$ also implies $d)$ is immediate.

When every quotient of a submodule of $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^k(\mathrm{M})$ is ideally separated for $\mathfrak{J}$, it results from \sref{IV.19.5.5} that condition $b)$ implies that $\mathbf{f}$ is $(\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^*(\mathrm{M}))$-regular; moreover, $(\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^*(\mathrm{M})\otimes_{\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak K}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{L}))[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_n]$ is isomorphic to $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{L}^*(\mathrm{M})$ and hence is flat over $\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{L}$. Its degree-zero direct summand $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^*(\mathrm{M})\otimes_{\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak K}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{L})$ is therefore flat; this proves that $b)$ implies $c)$. On the other hand, under the same hypothesis, $c)$ implies $a)$ by virtue of $(\mathbf{0},15.1.21)$ (where we can replace the Noetherian hypothesis by the hypothesis that $\mathrm{M}$ is ideally separated for $\mathfrak{J}$, by virtue of $(\mathbf{0}_\mathrm{III},10.2.1)$).

It remains therefore to prove that when $\mathrm{A}$ is Noetherian, $\mathrm{M}$ of finite type, and the $f_i$ belong to the radical of $\mathrm{A}$, $d)$ implies $a)$.

There exists a free $\mathrm{A}$-module $\mathrm{N}$ of \emph{finite type} and an exact sequence $0\to\mathrm{R}\to\mathrm{N}\to\mathrm{M}\to 0$. It suffices to prove that $d)$ implies $b)$, since every quotient of a sub-$\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^k(\mathrm{M})$ is then ideally separated for $\mathfrak{J}$, by virtue of the fact that $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^k(\mathrm{M})$ is a finitely generated $\mathrm{A}$-module, that $\mathfrak{J}$ is contained in the radical of $\mathrm{A}$, and that $\mathrm{A}$ is Noetherian (Bourbaki, \emph{Alg.~comm.}, chap.~III, \textsection~5, n\textsuperscript{o}~1); in other words, it suffices to show that $\psi_\mathrm{M}$ is \emph{bijective}.

As $\mathrm{N}$ is free and the sequence $\mathbf{f}$ is $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^*(\mathrm{A})$-regular by hypothesis, it is also $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^*(\mathrm{N})$-regular. One can therefore apply \sref{IV.19.6.3}, since every quotient module of a sub-$\operatorname{gr}_k(\mathrm{M})$ is separated for the $\mathfrak{J}$-preadic topology, the $f_i$ belonging to the radical of $\mathrm{A}$ by hypothesis. The diagram \sref{IV.19.6.2.1} here reads
\[
\xymatrix{
  & 0 \ar[d] \\
  (\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^*(\mathrm{R},\mathrm{N})\otimes_{\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak K}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{L}))[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_n] \ar[r]^-{\psi_\mathrm{R}'} \ar[d] & \operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{L}^*(\mathrm{R},\mathrm{N}) \ar[d] \\
  (\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^*(\mathrm{N})\otimes_{\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak K}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{L}))[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_n] \ar[r]^-{\psi_\mathrm{N}} \ar[d] & \operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{L}^*(\mathrm{N}) \ar[d] \\
  (\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^*(\mathrm{M})\otimes_{\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak K}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{L}))[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_n] \ar[r]^-{\psi_\mathrm{M}} \ar[d] & \operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{L}^*(\mathrm{M}) \ar[d] \\
  0 & 0
}
\tag{19.7.1.1}
\label{IV.19.7.1.1}
\]
where $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^*(\mathrm{R},\mathrm{N})$ (resp. $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{L}^*(\mathrm{R},\mathrm{N})$) is the graded associated to $\mathrm{R}$ for the filtration $\mathrm{R}\cap\mathfrak K^m\mathrm{N}$ (resp. $\mathrm{R}\cap\mathfrak{L}^m\mathrm{N}$); recall that in this diagram the columns are exact and that $\psi_\mathrm{N}$ is \emph{bijective}.

\end{enumerate}
\end{corollary}

Lorsque tout quotient d'un sous-module d'un $\operatorname{gr}_{\mathfrak K}^p(M)$ \textup{($p\geq0$)} est idéalement séparé pour $\mathfrak J$ \textup{(Bourbaki, \emph{Alg. comm.}, chap. III, § 5, n\textsuperscript{o} 1)}, les conditions \textup{a)}, \textup{b)} et \textup{c)} sont équivalentes.

Enfin, lorsque $A$ est noethérien, que les $f_i$ appartiennent au radical de $A$, que la suite $\mathbf f$ est $\operatorname{gr}_{\mathfrak K}^\bullet(A)$-régulière et que $M$ est un $A$-module de type fini, les conditions \textup{a)}, 

=================== PAGE IV-4 213 ===================
----- (English page IV-4 212: shown above in this file) -----
----- (English page IV-4 213: shown above in this file) -----
----- English, source_aligned/ega4-19.tex, page IV-4 214 -----
\oldpage[IV-4]{214}

\begin{env}[19.7.1.2]
\label{IV.19.7.1.2}
Set $\mathrm{B}=\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{R}^*\mathrm{A}\otimes_{\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak K}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{L})$, $\mathrm{P}=\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{R}^*(\mathrm{N})\otimes_{\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak K}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{L})$, $\mathrm{Q}=\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{L}^*(\mathrm{R},\mathrm{N})$; since $\psi_\mathrm{N}$ is bijective, $\mathrm{P}[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_n]$ identifies with $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{L}^*(\mathrm{N})$, and $\mathrm{Q}$ is a sub-$\mathrm{B}[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_n]$-module of $\mathrm{P}[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_n]$; we will first show that we have
\end{env}
\begin{env}[19.7.1.3]
\label{IV.19.7.1.3}
\[
  \mathrm{Q}=(\mathrm{Q}\cap\mathrm{P})[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_n].
\]
\end{env}

For this, set $\mathrm{Z}=\mathrm{Q}/((\mathrm{Q}\cap\mathrm{P})[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_n])$.
This is a sub-$\mathrm{B}[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_n]$-module of
\[
  \begin{aligned}
  &\mathrm{P}[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_n]
  /((\mathrm{Q}\cap\mathrm{P})[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_n])\\
  &\qquad=(\mathrm{P}/(\mathrm{Q}\cap\mathrm{P}))[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_n].
  \end{aligned}
\]
As an $(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{L})$-module, it is therefore isomorphic to a submodule of a direct sum of $(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{L})$-modules isomorphic to $\mathrm{P}/(\mathrm{Q}\cap\mathrm{P})=(\mathrm{P}+\mathrm{Q})/\mathrm{Q}$.
But $(\mathrm{P}+\mathrm{Q})/\mathrm{Q}$ is an $(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{L})$-module isomorphic to a submodule of $\mathrm{P}[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_n]/\mathrm{Q}$, which is none other than $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{L}^*(\mathrm{M})$ by the diagram \sref{IV.19.7.1.1}.

\begin{equation}
  \operatorname{Ass}_{\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{L}}(\mathrm{Z})\subset\operatorname{Ass}_{\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{L}}(\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{L}^*(\mathrm{M})).
  \tag{19.7.1.4}\label{IV.19.7.1.4}
\end{equation}

But each $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{L}^m(\mathrm{M})$ is by hypothesis a \emph{flat} $(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{L})$-module of finite type, hence projective since $\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{L}$ is Noetherian, and we therefore have $\operatorname{Ass}_{\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{L}}(\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{L}^*(\mathrm{M}))\subset\operatorname{Ass}_{\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{L}}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{L})$. To see that $\mathrm{Z}=0$, it suffices by (Bourbaki, \emph{Alg.~comm.}, chap.~IV, \textsection~1, n\textsuperscript{o}~1, cor.~1 of prop.~2 and n\textsuperscript{o}~3, prop.~7) to see that for every $\mathfrak{q}\in\operatorname{Ass}_{\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{L}}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{L})$, $\mathrm{Z}_\mathfrak{q}=0$. But the ideals of $\operatorname{Ass}_{\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{L}}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{L})$ are of the form $\mathfrak{p}/(\mathfrak{L}/\mathfrak{R})$, where $\mathfrak{p}\in\operatorname{Ass}_{\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak K}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{L})$, and it comes down to the same to see that $\mathrm{Z}_\mathfrak{p}=0$ for every $\mathfrak{p}\in\operatorname{Ass}_{\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak K}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{L})$. Now, if $\mathfrak{p}=\mathfrak{r}/\mathfrak{R}$, where $\mathfrak{r}$ is a prime ideal of $\mathrm{A}$, then $(\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{R}^*(\mathrm{M}))_\mathfrak{p}=\operatorname{gr}_{\mathfrak K_\mathfrak{r}}^*(\mathrm{M}_\mathfrak{r})$ and $(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{L})_\mathfrak{p}=\mathrm{A}_\mathfrak{r}/\mathfrak{L}_\mathfrak{r}$, and the images of the $f_i$ in $\mathrm{A}_\mathfrak{r}$ form a $\operatorname{gr}_{\mathfrak K_\mathfrak{r}}^*(\mathrm{A}_\mathfrak{r})$-regular sequence $(\mathbf{0},15.1.14)$; applying to $\mathrm{A}_\mathfrak{r}$, $\mathfrak K_\mathfrak{r}$ and $\mathrm{M}_\mathfrak{r}$ the implication $a)\Rightarrow b)$ of the statement, we see from the hypothesis $d)$ that $\psi_{\mathrm{M}_\mathfrak{r}}$ is bijective, hence also $\psi_{\mathrm{R}_\mathfrak{r}}'$ by virtue of \sref{IV.19.6.3}; in other words, $\mathrm{Q}_\mathfrak{r}=\mathrm{Q}_\mathfrak{r}'[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_n]$, where we have set $\mathrm{Q}'=\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{R}^*(\mathrm{R},\mathrm{N})\otimes_{\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak K}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{L})$, which is here a sub-$\mathrm{B}$-module of $\mathrm{P}$; in particular $\mathrm{Q}_\mathfrak{r}'=\mathrm{Q}_\mathfrak{r}\cap\mathrm{P}_\mathfrak{r}$; hence finally $\mathrm{Z}_\mathfrak{r}=\mathrm{Z}_\mathfrak{p}=0$, which completes the demonstration of \sref{IV.19.7.1.3}.

\begin{env}[19.7.1.5]
\label{IV.19.7.1.5}
By virtue of \sref{IV.19.7.1.3}, to prove that $\psi_\mathrm{R}'$ is surjective, it suffices to show that every homogeneous element of degree $m$ of $\mathrm{Q}\cap\mathrm{P}$ is the image of an element of the same degree of $\mathrm{Q}'=\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{R}^*(\mathrm{R},\mathrm{N})\otimes_{\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak K}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{L})$. Now, the elements of degree $m$ of $\mathrm{P}$ identify (by $\psi_\mathrm{N}$) with the elements of $(\mathfrak K^m\mathrm{N}+\mathfrak{L}^{m+1}\mathrm{N})/\mathfrak{L}^{m+1}\mathrm{N}$; those which belong to $\mathrm{Q}$ identify with the elements of $((\mathrm{R}\cap\mathfrak{L}^m\mathrm{N})+\mathfrak{L}^{m+1}\mathrm{N})/\mathfrak{L}^{m+1}\mathrm{N}$; and the images of the elements of degree $m$ of $\mathrm{Q}'$ identify with the elements of $((\mathrm{R}\cap\mathfrak{R}^m\mathrm{N})+\mathfrak{L}^{m+1}\mathrm{N})/\mathfrak{L}^{m+1}\mathrm{N}$. It will therefore suffice to prove, for $m>0$, the inclusion
\[
  ((\mathrm{R}\cap\mathfrak{L}^m\mathrm{N})+\mathfrak{L}^{m+1}\mathrm{N})\cap(\mathfrak{R}^m\mathrm{N}+\mathfrak{L}^{m+1}\mathrm{N})\subset(\mathrm{R}\cap\mathfrak{R}^m\mathrm{N})+\mathfrak{L}^{m+1}\mathrm{N}.
\]
As $\mathfrak K\subset\mathfrak{L}$, we see immediately that the first member of this relation is equal to $(\mathrm{R}\cap(\mathfrak{R}^m\mathrm{N}+\mathfrak{L}^{m+1}\mathrm{N}))+\mathfrak{L}^{m+1}\mathrm{N}$, so that everything reduces to proving
\end{env}
\begin{env}[19.7.1.6]
\label{IV.19.7.1.6}
\[
  \mathrm{R}\cap(\mathfrak{R}^m\mathrm{N}+\mathfrak{L}^{m+1}\mathrm{N})\subset(\mathrm{R}\cap\mathfrak{R}^m\mathrm{N})+\mathfrak{L}^{m+1}\mathrm{N}.
\]
\end{env}

est \emph{bijectif}. Il s'agit, en vertu de (19.6.3), de prouver que $\psi_R'$ est \emph{surjectif}, ce qui va être fait en plusieurs 

=================== PAGE IV-4 214 ===================
----- (English page IV-4 213: shown above in this file) -----
----- (English page IV-4 214: shown above in this file) -----
----- English, source_aligned/ega4-19.tex, page IV-4 215 -----
\oldpage[IV-4]{215}
We will proceed by induction on $m$, assuming therefore \sref{IV.19.7.1.6} verified when $m$ is replaced by an integer $m'<m$. We will consider on the other hand, for $m$ fixed and for an integer $d\geqslant 0$, the relation
\begin{equation}
  \mathrm{R}\cap(\mathfrak{R}^m\mathrm{N}+\mathfrak{L}^{m+1}\mathrm{N})\subset(\mathrm{R}\cap\mathfrak{R}^d\mathrm{N}\cap\mathfrak{L}^m\mathrm{N})+\mathfrak{L}^{m+1}\mathrm{N}.
  \tag{$*_d$}
\end{equation}

It is clear that it suffices to demonstrate $(*_d)$ for $d=m$, and on the other hand $(*_0)$ is trivially true. We will prove $(*_d)$ by \emph{induction on $d$}; in other words, we suppose, for a $d\geqslant 0$ fixed (with $d<m$), that $(*_d)$ is true, and we wish to prove
\begin{equation}
  \mathrm{R}\cap(\mathfrak{R}^m\mathrm{N}+\mathfrak{L}^{m+1}\mathrm{N})\subset(\mathrm{R}\cap\mathfrak{R}^{d+1}\mathrm{N}\cap\mathfrak{L}^m\mathrm{N})+\mathfrak{L}^{m+1}\mathrm{N}.
  \tag{$*_{d+1}$}
\end{equation}

Consider for this, for an $h\geqslant 0$, the relation
\begin{equation}
  \mathrm{R}\cap(\mathfrak{R}^m\mathrm{N}+\mathfrak{R}^d\mathfrak{L}^h\mathrm{N})\subset(\mathrm{R}\cap(\mathfrak{R}^{d+1}\mathrm{N}+\mathfrak{R}^d\mathfrak{L}^h\mathrm{N})\cap\mathfrak{L}^m\mathrm{N})+\mathfrak{L}^{m+1}\mathrm{N}
  \tag{$**_{d+1,h}$}
\end{equation}

The hypothesis $(*_d)$ implies that $(**_{d+1,0})$ is true. We will prove by \emph{induction on $h$} that $(**_{d+1,h})$ is true for every $h\geqslant 0$. But we have

\begin{lemma}[19.7.1.7]
\label{IV.19.7.1.7}
Let $\mathrm{A}$ be a Noetherian ring, $\mathrm{N}$ a finitely generated $\mathrm{A}$-module, $\mathfrak{L}$ an ideal of $\mathrm{A}$, $\mathrm{E}$, $\mathrm{F}$ two submodules of $\mathrm{N}$. For every $k>0$ there exists $h>0$ such that $\mathrm{E}\cap(\mathrm{F}+\mathfrak{L}^h\mathrm{N})\subset(\mathrm{E}\cap\mathrm{F})+\mathfrak{L}^k\mathrm{N}$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Indeed, let $\varphi:\mathrm{N}\to\mathrm{N}/(\mathrm{E}\cap\mathrm{F})=\mathrm{N}_1$ be the canonical homomorphism, and set $\mathrm{E}_1=\varphi(\mathrm{E})$, $\mathrm{F}_1=\varphi(\mathrm{F})$, so that $\mathrm{E}_1\cap\mathrm{F}_1=0$ and $\varphi(\mathrm{E}\cap(\mathrm{F}+\mathfrak{L}^h\mathrm{N}))=\mathrm{E}_1\cap(\mathrm{F}_1+\mathfrak{L}^h\mathrm{N}_1)$.
We know $(\mathbf{0}_\mathrm{I},7.3.2)$ that there exists $h$ such that $(\mathrm{E}_1+\mathrm{F}_1)\cap\mathfrak{L}^h\mathrm{N}_1\subset\mathfrak{L}^k(\mathrm{E}_1+\mathrm{F}_1)$; if $h$ is thus chosen and if $x_1\in\mathrm{E}_1$ is such that there exists $y_1\in\mathrm{F}_1$ for which $x_1-y_1\in\mathfrak{L}^h\mathrm{N}_1$, we deduce $x_1-y_1\in\mathfrak{L}^k\mathrm{N}_1$, hence $\mathrm{E}_1\cap(\mathrm{F}_1+\mathfrak{L}^h\mathrm{N}_1)\subset\mathfrak{L}^k\mathrm{N}_1$; therefore $\mathrm{E}\cap(\mathrm{F}+\mathfrak{L}^h\mathrm{N})\subset\mathrm{E}\cap\mathrm{F}+\mathfrak{L}^k\mathrm{N}$, which proves the lemma.
\end{proof}

It suffices then to apply this lemma with $\mathrm{E}=\mathrm{R}\cap\mathfrak{L}^m\mathrm{N}$, $\mathrm{F}=\mathfrak{R}^{d+1}\mathrm{N}$ and to take $k=m+1$ to deduce that $(**_{d+1,h})$ implies $(*_{d+1})$.

We suppose therefore in all that follows that $(**_{d+1,h})$ \emph{is satisfied.}

\begin{env}[19.7.1.8]
\label{IV.19.7.1.8}
\emph{Demonstration of $(**_{d+1,h+1})$; First case: $h\leqslant m\setmin d$.}

Start with an element
\end{env}
\begin{env}[19.7.1.9]
\label{IV.19.7.1.9}
\[
  g\in\mathrm{R}\cap(\mathfrak{R}^{d+1}\mathrm{N}+\mathfrak{R}^d\mathfrak{L}^h\mathrm{N})\cap\mathfrak{L}^m\mathrm{N}
\]
\end{env}

congruent modulo $\mathfrak{L}^{m+1}\mathrm{N}$ to an element $g_1\in\mathrm{R}\cap(\mathfrak{R}^m\mathrm{N}+\mathfrak{L}^{m+1}\mathrm{N})$; we therefore have

\begin{env}[19.7.1.10]
\label{IV.19.7.1.10}
\[
  g\in\mathfrak{R}^m\mathrm{N}+\mathfrak{L}^{m+1}\mathrm{N}.
\]
\end{env}

It will suffice to prove that $g$ is also congruent modulo $\mathfrak{L}^{m+1}\mathrm{N}$ to an element $g_2\in\mathrm{R}\cap(\mathfrak{R}^{d+1}\mathrm{N}+\mathfrak{R}^d\mathfrak{L}^{h+1}\mathrm{N})$, for this will imply $g_2\in\mathfrak{L}^m\mathrm{N}$, since $g\in\mathfrak{L}^m\mathrm{N}$ and $g_2\setmin g\in\mathfrak{L}^{m+1}\mathrm{N}$. In the case $h\leqslant m\setmin d$ under consideration, it is enough to prove

\begin{env}[19.7.1.11]
\label{IV.19.7.1.11}
\[
  (\mathfrak{R}^m\mathrm{N}+\mathfrak{L}^{m+1}\mathrm{N})\cap\mathfrak{R}^d\mathrm{N}
  \subset\mathfrak{L}^{m-d+1}\mathfrak{R}^d\mathrm{N}+\mathfrak{R}^{d+1}\mathrm{N}.
\]
\end{env}

Indeed, relations \sref{IV.19.7.1.9} and \sref{IV.19.7.1.10} imply that $g$ belongs to the left-hand side of \sref{IV.19.7.1.11}; and since $h\leqslant m\setmin d$, we have $\mathfrak{L}^{m-d+1}\subset\mathfrak{L}^{h+1}$, which will establish $(**_{d+1,h+1})$. Moreover $\mathfrak{R}^m\mathrm{N}\subset\mathfrak{R}^d\mathrm{N}$, and the relation

\begin{env}[19.7.1.12]
\label{IV.19.7.1.12}
\[
  \mathfrak{L}^{m+1}\mathrm{N}\cap\mathfrak{R}^d\mathrm{N}
  \subset\mathfrak{L}^{m-d+1}\mathfrak{R}^d\mathrm{N}
\]
\end{env}

will imply \sref{IV.19.7.1.11}. Since $\mathrm{N}$ is a free $\mathrm{A}$-module of finite type, it is therefore enough to prove

\begin{env}[19.7.1.13]
\label{IV.19.7.1.13}
\[
  \mathfrak{L}^{m+1}\cap\mathfrak{R}^d\subset\mathfrak{L}^{m-d+1}\mathfrak{R}^d.
\]
\end{env}

It will suffice to prove that, in general, for $d<m$, one has

\begin{env}[19.7.1.14]
\label{IV.19.7.1.14}
\[
  \mathfrak{L}^{m+1}\cap\mathfrak{R}^d\subset\mathfrak{L}^{m-d+1}\mathfrak{R}^d+\mathfrak{R}^{d+1}.
\]
\end{env}

Indeed, since $\mathfrak{R}\subset\mathfrak{L}$, hence $\mathfrak{L}^{m-d+1}\mathfrak{R}^d\subset\mathfrak{L}^{m+1}$, the preceding relation, applied successively with $d,d+1,\ldots,m$, implies
\[
  \mathfrak{L}^{m+1}\cap\mathfrak{R}^d
  \subset\mathfrak{L}^{m-d+1}\mathfrak{R}^d+\mathfrak{R}^{m+1},
\]
whence \sref{IV.19.7.1.13}, since $\mathfrak{R}^{m+1}\subset\mathfrak{L}^{m-d+1}\mathfrak{R}^d$.

\begin{env}[19.7.1.15]
\label{IV.19.7.1.15}
As $\mathfrak{L}=\mathfrak{J}+\mathfrak{R}$, the relation \sref{IV.19.7.1.14} also reads
\[
\begin{aligned}
  &(\mathfrak{J}^{m+1}+\mathfrak{J}^m\mathfrak{R}+\ldots+\mathfrak{J}\mathfrak{R}^m+\mathfrak{R}^{m+1})\cap\mathfrak{R}^d\\
  &\qquad\subset\mathfrak{J}^{m-d+1}\mathfrak{R}^d+\mathfrak{J}^{m-d}\mathfrak{R}^{d+1}+\ldots+\mathfrak{J}\mathfrak{R}^m+\mathfrak{R}^{m+1}+\mathfrak{R}^{d+1}\\
  &\qquad=\mathfrak{J}^{m-d+1}\mathfrak{R}^d+\mathfrak{R}^{d+1}.
\end{aligned}
\]

We will show that this inclusion is itself a consequence of
\end{env}
\begin{env}[19.7.1.16]
\label{IV.19.7.1.16}
\[
  \mathfrak{R}^a\mathfrak{J}^b\cap\mathfrak{R}^{a+1}\subset\mathfrak{R}^{a+1}\mathfrak{J}^{b-1}
\]
\end{env}
valid for $a\geqslant 0$, $b\geqslant 1$. Indeed, it suffices, to prove \sref{IV.19.7.1.15}, to show that for $0<q\leqslant d$,
\begin{env}[19.7.1.17]
\label{IV.19.7.1.17}
\[
  (\mathfrak{J}^{m+1}+\mathfrak{J}^m\mathfrak{R}+\ldots+\mathfrak{J}\mathfrak{R}^m+\mathfrak{R}^{m+1})\cap\mathfrak{R}^d\subset(\mathfrak{J}^{m+1-q}\mathfrak{R}^q+\mathfrak{J}^{m-q}\mathfrak{R}^{q+1}+\ldots+\mathfrak{R}^{m+1})\cap\mathfrak{R}^d
\]
\end{env}
since for $q=d$, this will imply \sref{IV.19.7.1.15}. To prove \sref{IV.19.7.1.17}, proceed by induction on $q$, supposing the relation true for $q<d$. An element of the right-hand side corresponding to $q$ can be written $y+z$, with $y\in\mathfrak{J}^{m+1-q}\mathfrak{R}^q$ and $z\in\mathfrak{J}^{m-q}\mathfrak{R}^{q+1}+\ldots+\mathfrak{R}^{m+1}$. Since $y+z\in\mathfrak{R}^d\subset\mathfrak{R}^{q+1}$ and $z\in\mathfrak{R}^{q+1}$, we have $y\in\mathfrak{R}^{q+1}$; by \sref{IV.19.7.1.16}, $y\in\mathfrak{J}^{m-q}\mathfrak{R}^{q+1}$, and hence
\[
  y+z\in\mathfrak{J}^{m-q}\mathfrak{R}^{q+1}+\ldots+\mathfrak{R}^{m+1},\quad\text{and}\quad y+z\in\mathfrak{R}^d
\]
by hypothesis, which proves \sref{IV.19.7.1.17} where $q$ has been replaced by $q+1$.

It remains to establish \sref{IV.19.7.1.16}. The case $b=1$ is trivial; suppose $b\geq2$. An element of the left-hand side is written $a_1f_1+\ldots+a_nf_n$, with $a_i\in\mathfrak K^a\mathfrak J^{b-1}$, and its image in $Q=\mathfrak K^a/\mathfrak K^{a+1}$ is zero. Since the sequence $\mathbf f$ is $Q$-regular, the Koszul relations allow us to choose $c_{ij}\in\mathfrak K^a\mathfrak J^{b-2}$, with $c_{ij}=-c_{ji}$ and $c_{ii}=0$, such that $a_i-\sum_j c_{ij}f_j\in\mathfrak K^{a+1}$.\footnote{Correction note. The required filtration bound is proved as follows. Put $I=\mathfrak J$, $s=b-1$ and $K_\bullet=K_\bullet(\mathbf f;Q)$, with differential $\delta$. For $u\in I^sK_1$ with $\delta_1u=0$, regularity gives $u=\delta_2C$. By $(\mathbf{0},15.1.7,15.1.9)$, $\operatorname{gr}_I(Q)\simeq(Q/IQ)[T_1,\ldots,T_n]$; the Koszul complex of the indeterminates on this module is exact in positive degrees. If $C\in I^dK_2$ with $d<s-1$, its degree-$d$ class is a cycle. For $d=0$, this class is zero, since a preimage under $\delta_3$ would have coefficient degree $-1$. For $d\geq1$, it is the boundary of a class of coefficient degree $d-1$; lifting that class to $D\in I^{d-1}K_3$ and replacing $C$ by $C-\delta_3D$ gives $C\in I^{d+1}K_2$, without changing $\delta_2C$. After at most $s-1$ steps, $C\in I^{s-1}K_2$. Lifting its coefficients to $\mathfrak K^a\mathfrak J^{b-2}$ gives the required $c_{ij}$. No passage to the completion is needed. The printed text incorrectly concludes that the originally chosen coefficients necessarily belong to $\mathfrak K^{a+1}$.} Replace the $a_i$ by these new coefficients: the antisymmetric terms cancel, so the sum $a_1f_1+\ldots+a_nf_n$ is unchanged, while all its coefficients belong to $\mathfrak K^a\mathfrak J^{b-1}\cap\mathfrak K^{a+1}$. It now suffices to argue by
